On the server: the lead's reviewer reads `tools/review_packet.py <G> <item> --stage 2` (node statement, dependencies' declarations, the file, the declarations it uses with their nodes, the compile), answers above all whether the Lean says exactly what the node says, hypothesis by hypothesis and clause by clause, and lands with `SHEAF_LEAD=L-only python3 tools/merge_delivery.py <G> <item> --stage 2 --reviewer <id> --head <sha> [--apply-fixes DIR]` (which runs `tools/land.sh`) or returns with the same tool's `return`; the global build `tools/build.py` verifies landings (first build GREEN in 10 s at 04:52). One definition per notion: 603 nodes with a source declaration carry it as `lean` and are not items. The lead writes no statements while deliveries wait.

**STAGE 2 PARKING (2026-10-11 05:53 UTC; launcher's order 05:50).** An item abandoned in stage 2 (`abandon:` release after 05:05 UTC) is parked for EVERY group until the lead resolves it: `tools/claim.py next --stage 2` skips it and `take` refuses it; `tools/claim.py parked` lists them; the list with reasons and resolutions is `coord/stage2-parked.md`. Convention nodes (a definition node that introduces no notion: universes, skeletons, scheme conventions, collections of references) are recorded as `lean = {existing: true, declaration: null, module: null, convention: true, by: "L", date, note}` and count as stated (`dag_queue.has_decl`, `claim.py`): five applied (def-weil-pbc-scheme-conventions, def-weil-size-convention, def-weil-scheme-morphism-properties, def-pc-morphism-conventions, def-sp-etale-dictionary). **STAGE 2 EXTRA NODES (decision of the human, 05:55 UTC; tools being extended, the line `STAGE 2 EXTRA NODES OK` follows when they accept it).** A stage-2 worker no longer abandons for a missing or unusable dependency: (A) a MISSING NOTION gets a NEW DEFINITION NODE in the same delivery, with its own module and `lean` record, cited by the item (the item's `stmt_deps` gains it: the one other field of the item a delivery may change); (B) a dependency whose recorded declaration or Mathlib match does not give the notion as the node states it gets, in the same delivery, a module writing that specialization as a Lean definition for the DEPENDENCY node, whose `lean` the delivery sets (its `mathlib_match` stays, with a note); (C) abandon only when the item's own statement is wrong or cannot be typed even with (A) and (B), with the precise reason. RULE FOR A NEW DEFINITION NODE'S FIELDS: file `dag/nodes/<chapter of the item>/<id>.json`, `id` of the form `def-<chapter prefix>-<short-name>` not existing on sheaf-dag; `kind`: "definition"; `title`; `statement`: self-contained TeX introducing its own objects and citing the definition nodes of every notion it is built from; `source`: {document, place} with the Stacks tag and label (or the document in literature/); `stmt_deps`: the cited ids; `proof`: [], `proof_deps`: [], `proof_origin`: null; `mathlib_searches`: the three kinds of search recorded (LeanSearch; Loogle or LeanFinder; grep of Mathlib, of the source repository and of lean/Sheaf), each `{tool, query, results, date, verdict}`; `mathlib_match`: null (if the notion exists in Mathlib or the source it is not a new node: the item cites that declaration through an `existing: true` record on a new node ONLY when no DAG node carries it; prefer recording the existing declaration on a new node with `existing: true` and no module); `status`: "split" (a definition needs no proof; the reviewer reads it like a stage-1 new node and the merge sets `reviewed` and `reviewed_lean`); `created`: {by: "worker", item: <the item>, date, group: <G>}; `blueprint`: {is: null, from: {node: `python3 tools/blueprint_refs.py ancestor <item>`, place: the place in that blueprint proof the notion serves, via: the item's via with the item in front}, same_as: []}; `lean_audit`: {master: {state: none}, slopmaster2: {state: none}}; `chapter`, `unit`: the item's; `lean`: the record of brief §5 for its module (`existing` false) or an existing declaration (`existing` true, no module file). The report gains a section `## New nodes` (one paragraph per new node: statement, source, searches) and, for (B), `## Dependency modules` (which dependency node, why its record was unusable, what the module states).

**STAGE 2 EXTRA NODES OK (2026-10-11 06:06 UTC).** `tools/preflight_delivery.py --stage 2`, `tools/review_packet.py --stage 2` and `tools/merge_delivery.py --stage 2` accept a delivery with several modules and the extra node files of (A) and (B) (classified as the item, NEW nodes and DEPENDENCY nodes; modules compiled in sibling order, a module importing a sibling is reported 'deferred' by the preflight and lands after its sibling; `coord/brief-stage2.md` §1, §5, §6 and `coord/delivery-errors.md` H1, H2, H12, H13 say it). The exact rule for a new definition node's fields is the paragraph STAGE 2 EXTRA NODES above, as implemented: file `dag/nodes/<chapter of the item>/<id>.json`, id `def-<prefix>-<name>` absent on the base; keys exactly `id, kind, title, statement, source, stmt_deps, proof, proof_deps, proof_origin, mathlib_searches, mathlib_match, status, created, blueprint, lean_audit, chapter, unit, lean`; kind "definition", status "split", non-empty title and TeX statement citing in `stmt_deps` every `[id]` it names (ids on the base or new in the delivery); `source` {document, place}; `proof` [], `proof_deps` [], `proof_origin` null, `mathlib_match` null; `mathlib_searches`: entries {tool, query, results, date, verdict} covering leansearch, loogle-or-leanfinder, grep; `created` {by: "worker", item: <item>, group: <G>, date}; `blueprint` {is: null, from: {node, place (not 'unspecified'), via} as `tools/blueprint_refs.py` computes for the item, same_as: []}; `lean_audit` {master: none, slopmaster2: none}; `chapter`, `unit` = the item's; `lean` as brief §5 (a node with existing: false has its module). A dependency node (B) is in the item's base `stmt_deps` and changes only in `lean` (existing: false, module, non-empty `note`). The item may change only `lean` and `stmt_deps` (added ids = new nodes). The items parked for reasons (A)/(B) are back in the queue (coord/stage2-parked.md).
**STAGE 2 STATEMENT CORRECTION (decision of the human, launcher 07:25 +08 = 2026-10-11 06:22 UTC written; tools being extended, the line `STAGE 2 STATEMENT CORRECTION OK` follows when they accept it).** Case (C) is no longer an abandonment: when the item's own statement is wrong or cannot be written (false, ill-typed, ambiguous, or leaving out what is needed to state it: an object not introduced, an action or a structure not specified, a hypothesis the source assumes throughout), the worker CORRECTS THE NODE in the delivery and writes the Lean for the corrected statement. The correction makes the node say what the SOURCE says (the Stacks tag, the blueprint unit's intent), never something easier; a correction that would change a unit's own statement is written up for coord/HUMAN.md and that item alone is still released (`abandon: unit statement: ...`). FIELD RULE FOR A CORRECTED ITEM: the item's node file may change in `statement`, and only as far as the correction forces it in `stmt_deps`, `proof` and `proof_deps` (plus `lean`, and `stmt_deps` gaining new nodes as before); it gains `statement_change` = {date, by: "<G> stage 2", old_statement: <the old TeX verbatim>, what: <what was wrong: the counterexample, or what could not be typed and why>, source: {document, place} <the source place the correction follows>, citers: [{id, holds: true|false, note: one line saying whether that citer's use of the item still holds}] <every node whose stmt_deps or proof_deps name the item, from a grep of dag/nodes; a citer that breaks is named, not edited>, and old_stmt_deps / old_proof / old_proof_deps when those changed}. The report gets a section `## Statement corrected` with the same content. A statement changed without `statement_change` is a fault (H7); a recorded correction is not. The reviewer reads the corrected statement against the source first, then the Lean against the corrected statement, and checks two of the citers. The parked items whose reason is of this kind go back to the queue (coord/stage2-parked.md, sorted once).
**STAGE 2 STATEMENT CORRECTION OK (2026-10-11 06:28 UTC).** `tools/preflight_delivery.py --stage 2`, `tools/review_packet.py --stage 2` and `tools/merge_delivery.py --stage 2` accept a CORRECTED item (`coord/brief-stage2.md` §1 (C), §2, §5, §6, `coord/delivery-errors.md` H7/H7b and `tools/README.md` "Stage 2" say it). The item is corrected when its `statement`, `proof`, `proof_deps` or `statement_change` differ from the base; it may then change in `statement`, `stmt_deps` (losing ids, gaining base or new nodes; every `[id]` of the corrected statement in it), `proof`, `proof_deps`, `statement_change` (`node_fields.py` STAGE2_ITEM_CORRECTION), besides `lean`; proof or proof_deps changed without a statement change, or a new statement_change on an unchanged statement, fails. `statement_change` (`node_fields.check_statement_change`): keys exactly `date` (YYYY-MM-DD), `by` ("<G> stage 2"), `old_statement` (the base statement verbatim), `what` (non-empty), `source` {document, place}, `citers` [{id, holds: true|false, note}] = exactly the base nodes whose stmt_deps or proof_deps contain the item; `old_stmt_deps` / `old_proof` / `old_proof_deps` present iff that field changed, verbatim from the base; `previous` = the base node's statement_change whole, present iff the base had one (an older stage-1 record left untouched is not checked). The report needs a non-empty `## Statement corrected` naming every citer. The preflight prints `item corrected: <n> citers, <k> marked holds:false`; the packet adds `## Statement corrected` before the Lean (old/new statement verbatim, what, source, the stmt_deps/proof/proof_deps diff, the citers table, the statements of two citers, holds:false first); the merge copies the five fields from the branch (refused if they moved on sheaf-dag since the base), writes `reviewed_lean` with `"statement_corrected": true`, and `--apply-fixes DIR/<item>.statement-change.json` replaces the record (checked again). Tested on a synthetic delivery (preflight OK, packet, merge --dry-run; missing statement_change and an incomplete citers list fail).

**LOCK TIMEOUT, SHEAF-DAG RECORDS, LET IN SIGNATURES (07:19 UTC).** `merge_delivery.py` and `attach.py` (the only tools that take coord/.merge.lock; `land.sh`/`landing.py` hold local/tree.lock) now give up after SHEAF_LOCK_TIMEOUT (120) s with `coord/.merge.lock held for 120 s by pid <n>: <command line>` read from /proc/locks (tools/mergelock.py; a stage-2 dry run takes the lock too), so a reviewer's `flock` around them fails after 2 minutes instead of stopping every merge. Both briefs say these tools are NEVER wrapped in `flock`; `flock coord/.merge.lock -c …` is only for a plain git add/commit. An `existing: true` record may name a declaration already landed in lean/Sheaf (module `Sheaf.<…>`, branch `sheaf-dag`, no `file`), for the item, a new node or a dependency node of (B): `check_existing` greps lean/Sheaf/<path>.lean on the base and nothing is compiled for it. coord/brief-stage2.md §1/§5 say so; lem-pc-tower-enough-injectives (rejected for `CategoryTheory.roosTowerEnoughInjectives`) is un-parked. `stage2_delivery.declarations()` no longer reads the `:=` of a `letI`/`let`/`haveI`/`have`/`suffices`/`obtain` at depth 0 after the signature's colon as the proof start (the binder ends at `;` or at a line indented no deeper than its keyword). **MAIN SESSION DISPATCHES ONLY (2026-10-11 07:39 UTC; decision of the human, launcher 16:40 +08).** The main session does no long task: it only receives launcher messages, deliveries and sub-agents' results, and dispatches sub-agents, in turns of a few tool calls with no reading of node files or Lean, no JSON repair, no statement writing, no tool development and no long shell command. Every piece of work (reviews that merge or return themselves, tool fixes one background sub-agent at a time, parked-item sorting, bookkeeping) is a sub-agent's, and when one finishes the main session starts the next on the oldest waiting thing. A main turn longer than five minutes is a fault the launcher interrupts (roles/maintainer.md, "The main session only dispatches").
**TIERED REVIEW (2026-10-11 23:05 +08, human):** stage-2 deliveries are classified by `tools/review_class.py <G> <item>` / `review_class.py list` as FULL (a definition, new definition node, dependency module, several modules, corrected statement or changed stmt_deps/proof, `existing: true`, STATEMENT-DISPUTED, repair of a return, failed automatic landing, or the one-in-four hash sample of statements) or AUTO (every other delivery: the plain statement of a lemma with sorry). AUTO deliveries land without a reviewer via the background loop `~/cc1/autoland.py` (`merge_delivery.py <G> <item> --stage 2 --reviewer AUTO --head <sha> --verdict PASS`, log `~/cc1/autoland.log`; `reviewed_lean.reviewer` = "AUTO" for a later audit; a refusal goes to `local/autoland-failed.tsv` and the delivery becomes FULL). The lead launches reviewers only on FULL branches (oldest first, repaired returns first), five slots, eight only while more than ten FULL wait (SETUP.md, roles/maintainer.md §7 (4d), tools/README.md).
**STAGE 2 ORDER: BLOCKERS FIRST (2026-10-11 23:30 +08, human):** among the open stage-2 items the definitions still come first, then every item by the number of stage-2 nodes still needing a statement that cannot be stated before it (counted transitively through stmt_deps, each once), largest first, ties by the distance order (`tools/dag_queue.py`, `tools/queue.py`). coord/queue.tsv prints that number as its last column `blockers`; claimed items keep their claims, only the order of what is open changes.

**NON-VANISHING LOCUS X_s (07:59 UTC; decision of L).** The non-vanishing locus X_s of a global section s of an invertible module M (or of `M.tensorPow n`) is `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Modules.nonvanishingLocus M s` (lean/Sheaf/AlgebraicGeometry/Ample.lean, node def-weil-pbc-ample-invertible-module): K1's germ condition, the germ of s at x lies outside maximalIdeal • ⊤ of the stalk, equal by definition to the predicate written inline in `IsAmple`. **BASE CHANGE X_T (08:05 UTC; decision of L).** The base-changed scheme X_T of f : X ⟶ S along T : Over S (node def-pc-base-change, lean/Sheaf/AlgebraicGeometry/BaseChangeConventions.lean, merge 9b19482bd) is `CategoryTheory.Limits.pullback f T.hom` (= `Over.pullback`); the base-changed morphism f_T : X_T ⟶ T is `pullback.snd f T.hom` and the projection X_T ⟶ X is `pullback.fst f T.hom` (corrected 11:39 UTC, M1 66136964e; the earlier "f_T = pullback.fst" was wrong); g_X is `baseChangeMap`, induced sections are `sectionBaseChange`, the geometric fibre is `Scheme.Hom.geometricFiberAt`, the fibre over t is `(pullback.snd f T.hom).fiber t`. The record's main declaration `Scheme.Modules.moduleBaseChange` is only module base change; citers needing X_T use the pullback directly. Amended 15:21 UTC (B-1521): both factor orders are accepted — `pullback f T.hom` with `pullback.snd` and `pullback i f'` with `pullback.fst` (G1 ff7ae1fe5, R-tlc); a cartesian square may also be an `IsPullback` witness (CARTESIAN SQUARES IN sec-proper-base-change).

**DERIVED RESTRICTION TO AN OPEN (08:15 UTC; superseded 14:18 UTC, B-1418; decision of L).** For a scheme X with modules `SheafOfModules (AlgebraicGeometry.moduleRings X.toRingedSpace)`, derived restriction to an open U lands in `schemeDerivedCategory U.toScheme` and is `(SheafOfModules.overFunctor (moduleRings X.toRingedSpace) U ⋙ Equivalence.functor (C := …) (D := …) (Scheme.Modules.overEquiv U)).mapDerivedCategory` — the over-site restriction followed by the functor of `Scheme.Modules.overEquiv U`, then `mapDerivedCategory`; the equivalence is named with explicit `(C := …) (D := …)`, never through `show … from` (G1 ce180b4c0, M1 f526a6fea; used by G1 00e16b592, E1 534dc09bd, F1 4e129afd2, M1 35947b58d, J1 1daa3dc46). Overlaps U ⊓ V: `(Scheme.Modules.overEquiv U).inverse ⋙ overMap (homOfLE …) ⋙ (Scheme.Modules.overEquiv (U ⊓ V)).functor` (H1 8f2941a40; `Additive` of the three-fold composite needs `set_option synthInstance.maxSize 2048`). The earlier plain form `(SheafOfModules.overFunctor (moduleRings X) U).mapDerivedCategory` into `(moduleRings X).over U` is no longer the convention for new statements; its landed uses (IsPerfect in def-pc-perfect-complex, M1 8905f8d2e, and citers) are a repair candidate or a stage-3 bridge. 'Locally on X' statements whose predicate exists only on schemes (IsPerfect, schemeDerivedTensorCohomologyVanishes, HasLocallyFiniteTorDimension) may use `derivedPullback U.ι` onto U.toScheme (C1 b8d6461bd, J1 77d34db41); the bridge to the composite is stage 3. Between opens i : W ⟶ U the over-site restriction is `(SheafOfModules.overMap (moduleRings X) i).mapDerivedCategory`, and the Hom restriction is `AlgebraicGeometry.derivedOpenHomRestriction` (node lem-sc-derived-hom-mayer-vietoris, lean/Sheaf/Paper/DerivedHomMayerVietoris.lean, merge 453ada29d). Its sorry'd exactness instances `AlgebraicGeometry.moduleOverFunctor_additive`/`_limits`/`_colimits` and `AlgebraicGeometry.moduleOverMap_additive`/`_limits`/`_colimits` are THE shared ones: later items cite them, never restate them (H4). `moduleRings` was made a public abbrev (was `private`, used in public statements) so citers can name it. Amended 14:47 UTC (B-1447): for nodes stated on a RingedSpace (not a scheme) the plain over-site restriction `SheafOfModules.overFunctor (moduleRings X) W` into `(moduleRings X).over W`, then `mapDerivedCategory`, IS the correct form (K1 b7bd91fa9 derived Mayer–Vietoris; J1 6632eba7e uses plain `overFunctor` instead of an overEquiv composite on a ringed space); the overEquiv composite is for schemes only.

**CARTAN–EILENBERG RESOLUTIONS (08:44 UTC; decision of L).** A Cartan–Eilenberg resolution (consumers thm-sp-grothendieck, lem-pc-grothendieck-ss-double-complex-model, the CE comparison/homotopy lemmas, ce-horizontal-lift, ce-vertical-nullhomotopy, …) is stated in the form of E1's landed `CategoryTheory.exists_cartanEilenberg_resolution` (node lem-sp-cartan-eilenberg-exists, lean/Sheaf/CategoryTheory/CartanEilenbergExists.lean, merge 733ed5a76): `I : HomologicalComplex₂ C (ComplexShape.up ℤ) (ComplexShape.up ℕ)` with `IsZero (I.X p)` for p < 0; the augmentation is ONE bicomplex morphism `e : ((CochainComplex.single₀ C).mapHomologicalComplex (ComplexShape.up ℤ)).obj K ⟶ I`; columns are resolutions via the fields of `InjectiveResolution` (terms injective, `QuasiIso (e.f p)`); horizontal cycles/boundaries/cohomology are resolved via `HomologicalComplex.cyclesMap e p`, `image.map (Arrow.homMk' (e.f (p - 1)) (e.f p) (e.comm (p - 1) p))` and `HomologicalComplex.homologyMap e p` of the same `e` (Stacks convention, 015H). J1's landed lem-sp-cartan-eilenberg-properties (0f91f06a8) uses the exchanged-index convention its node prescribes; stage 3 bridges it by an index flip — no restatement now. Where a consumer needs H^q(G C^{p,•}) tied to the differential induced by d_I, it states a natural comparison isomorphism, not a bare `Nonempty`.

**FILE-LOCAL NOTATION (14:18 UTC; decision of L).** no file-local `notation`/`local notation`/macro, `private` definition or abbreviation in statements, and no `local instance`/`instance` that declares new data in a statement module: a recorded statement must read outside its file and namespace (F1 91b674ef5, J1 f048a280a, J1 13bfb5aba, C1 46a980a7c, E1 b5d12a0ed). `attribute [local instance]` of an existing declaration is allowed (LOCAL ATTRIBUTE INSTANCES).

A `local instance`/`instance` declaring NEW data in a statement file stays a fault (H4).

**RECURRING FAULTS (B-6, 15:50 UTC; for reviewers and second readers).** • instance lambdas / `toAlgebra` / inline `Module.compHom` in statements → `[Algebra]` binders or the STATE form; • tautological or derivable conjuncts (instances, rfl lemmas, naturality, a dependency's clause) → delete, state the node only (N1, T1); • hand-written presentations or predicates → `Algebra.Presentation`, the recorded predicate (`IsPseudoCoherent`, …); • unqualified names → statements must resolve outside their namespace; • data or instances from sorried theorems (`Classical.choose`, `.asEquivalence`, `.1`) → proved declarations or a `Nonempty`/∃ theorem (G1, H1, M1); • re-pointed or re-signed records of other nodes → revert, rule (B) only (U1, K1, S1, Q1); • untied existentials and free iso binders → tie to the canonical map (P1, K1, M1); • statement corrections without a named source error, or vacuous/H7 ones → revert (E1, R1, V1).

**Ownership of a shared missing notion (2026-10-11 06:08 UTC).** When several items of different groups need the same missing definition node, the FIRST group returned for it writes it and the others wait for its landing and then cite it (a notice in each group's returns file names the owner). Current: def-exc-discrete-modules-category is M1's (F1, K1, C1 wait).

**The unit route of the target** (`tools/units.py route`): rank 0 `sk-thm-properbasechange` (chapter sec-proper-base-change, route Stacks 095T, uses sec-curve-estimates, sec-nagata-compactification, sec-picard-framework); rank 1 its five cited units `sk-def-conv` (sec-nagata-compactification), `sk-def-exchange`, `sk-def-pbc`, `sk-thm-p1`, `sk-thm-reduction`; rank 2 `sk-def-br`, `sk-thm-curveestimate` (sec-curve-estimates), `sk-def-ia`, `sk-thm-chow`, `sk-thm-iff` (sec-proper-base-change), `sk-thm-groth-ss` (sec-picard-framework); rank 3 the Picard, Jacobian, Kummer, Hilbert 90, cohomological-dimension and abelian-variety units; and so on into sec-abelian-varieties. So the reach through curves, Picard and Jacobians is the unit route itself (the chapter says: follow the Stacks project, 095T, whose proof goes through curves), not an artefact of the import: those branches are kept. What the "text wins" import may have added are the proof edges of individual nodes that point outside their unit's route: `tools/units.py check` counts 1923 such edges in 151 units across the whole DAG (imported, unreviewed nodes). They are not pruned by guess: the split of the target and of its first two levels of units will show which of them the proof uses. Order of work: the queue is now ordered by unit rank, then distance, and only the route's units are worked.

**The three levels** (SETUP.md): the records are `dag/chapters/` and `dag/units/` (`tools/units.py build`), every node carries `chapter` and `unit`, and the checks are in `check_dag.py` and `review_split.py` (`stages/1-dag.md` §5–6). **Check of the 93 items reviewed today against the rule:** 10 of them have a proof dependency outside their unit's route (13 distinct edges, `tools/units.py check`), all in the old target's area except one: `thm-weil-final-betti`, `-integrality`, `-rh`, `-functional-equation` (each of the five assembled theorems uses another of the five, which their units do not cite: the blueprint's own assembly, not a worker's invention), `prop-cohomological-finiteness` (uses the proper/compact-support coincidence of sk-input-weights), `prop-trace-determinant` (uses prop-cohomological-finiteness), `lem-weil-frobenius-cohomology-transport-iso` (uses two open-square lemmas of sk-d-weil-push), and, in the new target's own unit, `cor-weil-pbc-geometric-fiber-finite`, whose proof uses `lem-kun-leray-bdd` (composition of derived functors, a tool lemma filed in a Künneth unit). None of these is a change of route in the mathematical sense: they are uses of generic tool lemmas or of sibling theorems that the unit text takes for granted; none was flagged at review because the rule did not exist. The lead has asked the human (`coord/HUMAN.md`) whether a generic tool lemma filed in another unit counts as a departure; until the answer, such uses are reported by the tools and judged at review, and the one in the target's unit will be revisited when `sk-thm-properbasechange` is realized.

**Known defects of the import, to be read before trusting a node** (the ledger has the history): the `uses_not_cited` and `cites_outside_uses` flags mark edges the text and the frontmatter disagree on, and 42 text-only citations inside cycles were pointers (now in `pointers`); imported proofs are often a list of the propositions behind a clause, not a proof (every reviewed item so far had hidden steps); definitions assert theorems inside them; the import filed many nodes in the wrong unit (grab-bag nodes such as `def-weil-scheme-morphism-properties`, `def-weil-pbc-scheme-conventions` define a dozen notions each); the same notion is defined in several chapters (constructible subsets, Tor amplitude, derived tensor, extension by zero, K-injective resolutions, separably closed fields) and the workers point to the reviewed one and report the rest; `lean_audit` came from the blueprint's `lean` lists only, so sorry-free declarations that state a node exactly are regularly found by the workers and attached at review, and conversely a `closed` node may be closed on a declaration that states something else (def-weil-pbc-property was: rejected names go into `lean_names_rejected`); the key node of every unit on the route is read against its Lean when the unit is realized, closed or not; the blueprint's Stacks tags are sometimes wrong (six were corrected on one node); `proof_deps` entries without a step and steps with `elementary: null` are unjudged. Unit-level gap found 2026-10-09: the parenthetical justification of finiteness of the symmetric-product morphism in step 5 of sk-thm-reduction (integrality on the charts) is incomplete for n >= 2; the claim is right and is proved another way at the node level (lem-weil-pbc-symmetric-product-finite). Literature gap found 2026-10-09: Stacks Lemma 0GJA ("iff" for the dense-open reduction of the eight curve assertions) fails in one direction for mixed torsion sheaves; the node lem-weil-pbc-curve-assertions-dense-open states that direction under the hypothesis "p-primary or prime to p", which all uses satisfy.

The DAG node lemma-weil-pbc-constructible-approximation now states the morphism form Stacks 09ZE uses. Entry in coord/HUMAN.md.
- **Literature gap, proof level (2026-10-09 16:26):** Stacks 09Z6 (constructible sheaves embed into finite pushforwards of constant sheaves on a Noetherian scheme) justifies 'finitely many points suffice' by the ascending chain condition 09YV, but the kernels in question shrink and constructible sheaves have no descending chain condition (on Spec Z with Lambda = Z/2, extensions by zero from complements of more and more primes form a strictly descending chain); lem-weil-pbc-constructible-module-finite-pushforward-noetherian uses instead the partition argument Stacks offers in the same sentence (lem-weil-pbc-constructible-noetherian-finite-selection). The statement is unaffected.
- **Literature gap (2026-10-09 15:57):** Stacks' proof of Theorem 09YQ (etale cohomology of a limit of qcqs schemes with affine transition maps) reduces to Lemma 09YP, which computes cohomology over objects of the colimit of the affine etale sites; a non-affine X_i is not an object there, so the proof as written covers affine X_i only. The node lem-weil-pbc-etale-cohomology-limit-of-schemes supplies the reduction of the qcqs case to the affine case by Mayer-Vietoris (agent-written steps 5-8, with a new node for the compatibility of Mayer-Vietoris with pullback); the route uses only the affine case.
- **Lean statements false as stated (2026-10-09 15:43):** the source repository's `IsConstructibleSheaf` has locally closed strata without constructibility, so `Etale.exists_mono_biproduct_lowerStar` (09Z7), `exists_isNoetherian_upperStar_iso` and `exists_isNoetherian_mono_biproduct_lowerStar` are false (counterexample in coord/HUMAN.md); the earlier attachment of the first to lem-weil-pbc-constructible-module-finite-pushforward-embedding was withdrawn to `lean_candidates`; the Noetherian case and the closed pullback lemma `exists_mono_biproduct_lowerStar_upperStar` are unaffected.
- **Imported statement false (2026-10-09 15:40):** prop-weil-pbc-gabber-affine-comparison (sk-thm-p1, imported, unreviewed) asserts Gabber's comparison H^q(X, F) = H^q(X_0, F_0) for every affine X of finite type over a henselian pair (A, I); only the pair form X = Spec A (Stacks 09ZI) holds (counterexamples: A = k[[t]], X = Spec A[x, 1/x, 1/(x-t)], F = Z/l; the Artin-Schreier cover in the docstring of the source repository's Gabber.lean:217). The imported proof of lemma-weil-pbc-injective-restriction-acyclicity rested on it; the split follows Stacks 0A51 (henselization of the pair, pair-form Gabber, limit theorem, Mayer-Vietoris) and the pair form is the new node prop-weil-pbc-gabber-pair-comparison. The false node is flagged unreachable with a lead note.
- **Imported definition too weak (2026-10-09 15:58):** def-weil-pbc-finite-torsors (sec-abelian-varieties, imported) defines an M-torsor without the local-sections condition of Stacks 03AH (2), so the empty sheaf is a torsor and thm-galois-torsor-h1 (imported, sk-prop-input-torsors) is false as stated. lemma-weil-pbc-h1-torsor-comparison states the condition explicitly in its step; the definition and the theorem's vague "natural in (Y, M)" are to be fixed when they are split (the precise naturality needed is recorded in that node's review note and the ledger).
- **Imported statement false in general (2026-10-09 15:05):** lem-fet-locally-trivial (sec-curve-estimates, imported, unit of the finite-etale points) asks for one surjective etale Y' → Y and one finite set S trivializing a finite etale P → Y; false for disconnected Y (Y = pt ⊔ pt, P one point over one component), true for connected Y. Found by the worker of lem-weil-pbc-finite-etale-trace-degree, which used Stacks 04HN instead. To be corrected at its own split (add 'Y connected' or allow S to vary with the component).

**REVIEW MODE STANDING** (2026-10-10 14:33 UTC, human's order via the launcher): the eight remote groups are run by a dispatcher script (no leads), 40 workers at all times, returns first; deliveries expected to rise from ~50/h to well over 100/h. Attachments arrive as releases `abandon: attachment <item>: <declaration>, <file>:<line>` in coord/deliveries.log; L verifies transitive sorry-freeness (sorryfree) and the statement, and closes the node or writes a notice to the group. **In progress** (2026-10-09 22:29 UTC): the review-commit-claim loop on the queue of the target, five opus workers at effort medium (items claimed with `claim.py next L --stage 1` in queue order; claims in `coord/claims/`); one commit per reviewed item, per-item timings in `local/target-stats.txt` (opus-relaunch6 lines). Remote groups C1, D1, E1, F1, G1 (5 claims each; G1 holds 6) deliver on deliver/<group>/<item> and are reviewed per roles/remote-lead.md; since 19:55 UTC the human's rule applies: a group with an open entry in coord/returns/<group>.md is refused new claims by `tools/claim.py next` until it repairs the item or abandons it with a reason starting with 'abandon:' (release is otherwise refused while a return entry or a delivery branch exists; merge, release and abandonment delete the entries, so the returns files are exact; abandonments are counted in the groups table); the merge tool runs only with SHEAF_LEAD=L-only in the lead's shell. Deliveries are checked after each review (`tools/merge_delivery.py list`) and a background watch of refs/heads/deliver/ runs. Since the decision of ~20:22 UTC a remote group cannot release an item with an open return entry or a delivery branch except by abandoning it ('abandon: <reason>', counted per group); a claim obtained through a bypass is released by the maintainer. Since ~22:22 UTC (decision of the human): group D1 is stopped (claims abandoned, branches deleted, returns cleared); the remote groups run tools/preflight_delivery.py on their clone before every push (one commit on the current tip, only the item's files, a real group review, new nodes without proof steps and with cited statements, verified Mathlib lines, steps citing nodes rather than quoting tags, review_split and check_dag); a delivery that fails it is not pushed. Open returns at 21:32 UTC: D1 sheaves-directed-colimit-sections, E1 complex-injective-resolution-mono and descend-separated, F1 etale-free-sheaf-constructible (third return), G1 stein-factorization-affine (second return) and unramified-etale-local-decomposition. Pending for a quiet moment: re-search with the new tools the unsplit new nodes created before the search rule (grep only); stage-3 merge candidates accumulate in the ledger (relative Gabber node, the two superseded finite-etale nodes, lem-weil-pbc-projective-space-twist-glued-identification vs the charts node's clause (v), lemma-weil-pbc-constructible-finite-pushforward vs its Lambda-module form, the two definitions of etale now reconciled by the two smooth-of-relative-dimension-0 items). The Wanted documents of `literature/LIST.md` (Weibel 2.4.6/2.2.8 added) and the questions in `coord/HUMAN.md` are open; nothing waits on them.

**Re-check of the Mathlib searches with the Lean tools (2026-10-09, evening; decision in SETUP.md, brief `coord/brief-recheck.md`, lists `coord/recheck-A..E.tsv`, results in commits d24daa2, 81d8617, 45f2588, c3882d6, 24de1c6).** 111 nodes of the target's reach, searched by grep only before the tools existed, were re-searched by five opus sub-agents (one list per group of units): 69 Mathlib leaves, 26 reviewed splits, 16 unsplit nodes created that day. Result: 64 leaves confirmed (declaration read in this Mathlib and compared clause by clause); 2 leaves withdrawn (def-pur-sheaf-tensor-product, def-pc-module-pullback: both define a construction by a formula where Mathlib defines it as a left adjoint, and the agreement is a theorem; both are back in the queue as unsplit with the old match kept under `mathlib_match_withdrawn`); 4 matches corrected in their `how` without change of declaration (def-weil-sheaf-of-modules-category, def-weil-triangulated-category, def-weil-constructible-subset, def-weil-regular-scheme), several more made precise; 0 leaves gained; 0 splits changed; 0 new leaf nodes; 41 nodes unchanged. Every node now carries the three kinds of search. Noted for later items: lemma-weil-pbc-geometric-stalk-conservative may be a leaf (`Scheme.isConservative_pointSmallEtale`, if Mathlib's point fibres are the node's geometric stalks); def-sp-abelian and def-weil-t-structure look like leaves; the equivalence clauses inside def-weil-regular-scheme (02IT) and def-weil-constructible-subset (005H/005L) are claims inside definitions; `Sheaf.hasSeparator` for the separator half of lem-weil-pbc-sheaf-flat-separator. Tooling: Loogle (3 requests per 30 s) and LeanFinder rate-limit when five agents share them; lean_local_search answers "index: unavailable" (no language server running), so only its positive answers count; the LeanSearch and LeanFinder indexes lag this Mathlib (files of 2026 missing), grep covers them.

**Lead mistakes of the day, recorded so that the next session does not repeat them:** brief descriptions wrong about Karoubi completion, ConstantSheaf.lean, Stacks tags 0A9D/0G7G, the cartesian square in Refinement (morning); the cokernel of τ≤q-1 → τ≤q described as H^q(K)[-q] (it is only quasi-isomorphic to it); the forgetful functor Mod(X,R) → Ab described as the right adjoint of an exact functor (its left adjoint is not exact for R = F_r); several Stacks tags in briefs pointing to sections rather than lemmas; the claims of the first target batch hand-picked instead of taken from the queue. The workers caught each of these; a brief states what it is unsure of as a question, not as a fact.

Ledger lines of that window (written as '2026-10-09 16:20' … '2026-10-10 01:00') have the same defect; the commits carry the true times. From now on every time is read from `date -u`.

**LEAN REFRESH DONE** (2026-10-10 10:53 UTC; the human's decision relayed at 09:47, commit 6fde5cb5d). The done-branch checkout `~/weil-conjectures-slopmaster2` is at `71d63dcf` (from `1ad77be9`, 882 commits; same Mathlib, lake-manifest unchanged; `.lake/build` seeded from the other session's worktree, 10116 jobs built with 12 threads by 10:12). New sorry map `local/sorry_map_slopmaster2_71d63dcf.jsonl.gz`: 32094 declarations (21984 owners after merging auxiliaries; 420 with `sorry`, 271 of them named by no node), made with the `Weil` root included; the first run died with the session at the 21st account limit and was rerun detached (10:30–10:47). Names added to the audit: the `lean = [...]` lists of `upstream-blueprint-main` (849 lists, 508 for DAG nodes, 754 names; an old `blueprint_lean` name absent from the branch is `superseded` when the current list exists) and the docstring tags `(blueprint: <id>)`: 1438 tag ids; **822 declarations with such a tag matched a node** (exact tags for 473 nodes; qualified tags such as "the implication (1) ⇒ (2)" for 78 nodes, which count as partial and never close a node); 182 tagged ids are no DAG node, 75 tags sit outside a declaration docstring. Also a list of every declaration of the imported libraries (`local/library_names_mathlib.tsv.gz`, 473131 names, `tools/library_names.lean`), so that a Mathlib declaration named by the blueprint counts as found and sorry-free. The lean-lsp server already serves the new checkout (it finds the new declarations), no restart needed.

Numbers (the DAG as of the refresh, 4471 nodes; the "before" is the same DAG under the old audit): slopmaster2 states closed 384 → 623, closed_part 10 → 17, sorry_here 44 → 141, rests_on_sorry 64 → 114, none 3925 → 3576 (the earlier counts on the 09:51 DAG). On the target's reach: **nodes newly closed 154, of them 146 open (imported or unsplit) nodes, now finished and out of the queue**; the live frontier (nodes reached without passing a finished node) 2602 → 2553; stage-1 open nodes on the reach 668 (532 imported + 136 unsplit) → 627 (495 + 132); finished nodes on the full reach 178 → 331; the queue 956 → 861 items (113 left it, including those that lie only below newly closed nodes). Attachment check: after the partial-tag rule only 5 open nodes on the reach are `closed_part` (def-weil-pbc-primary-torsion, lem-pc-cartier-criterion, lem-pc-divisor-line-bundle-properties, lem-pc-hilb-addition, lem-pc-hilb-smooth): claimed by L and given to 5 workers as ordinary items with the attachment check first (`brief-attach.md`), before any more splitting; the 103 open nodes whose Lean has `sorry` (33 sorry_here, 70 rests_on_sorry) stay ordinary queue items (split following the Lean's own pieces). lemma-weil-pbc-five-lemma-transfer and thm-pd-spreading-out are closed (claim released). check_dag: 0 cycles, the 7 standing reach warnings.

**Attachment check done** (2026-10-10 11:04 UTC). Review lag (10:12 UTC): 11 deliveries waiting, the oldest since 09:36 UTC (about 35 minutes), more than the twenty minutes the human set; the lead missed them while the delivery watch slept (10-minute polling) during the Lean refresh. Working through them now; the watch is now checked at every review.

**Pilot numbers, effort `xhigh` (batches 1–2, 2026-10-09, 05:53–07:20 UTC).** Two accounts hit their 5-hour session limits: the first after about an hour with 10 workers running, the second after 16 minutes (it had been used elsewhere). 11 items were delivered and passed review: 9 with a report, 2 found complete on disk after their worker died; 12 workers died mid-item, 7 of them leaving nothing usable. Per delivered item: 9.5–14 minutes of wall time (563–852 s, median ~720 s) and 119k–268k worker tokens (median ~170k); 0–6 new nodes per item (median 2), about half of them Mathlib leaves. The review rejected nothing mathematically; what it had to correct or note: two workers could not read the PDFs (no `pdftotext`; installed since) and wrote the proof as an agent's; one refused the review script because the brief forbade `git` (brief fixed); one died leaving its item citing nodes it had not written (brief now says new nodes first); one removed a pointer clause from a definition's statement (accepted, the slopmaster2 text has no such clause); claims inside definitions were kept as the source wrote them and reported.

**Pilot numbers, fable at effort `medium` (batch 3, started 07:32 UTC, human's decision in SETUP.md).** 16 items delivered and passed review, none rejected: wall time 102–679 s (median 282 s), 62k–207k worker tokens (median 98k), 0–7 new nodes per item (median 2.5, 45 in all). Compared with xhigh (median ~720 s, ~170k tokens): about a third of the time and half the tokens per item. The four statements with proofs took longer than the definitions (prop-functional-equation 441 s / 128k, prop-duality-reciprocity 679 s / 207k, thm-weil-final-rh 281 s / 111k, and the finishing pass on thm-weil-final 100 s / 66k); their proofs followed the source, named every step's uses, and isolated the hidden linear algebra as new lemma nodes, as the xhigh proofs did. What the review corrected: nothing mathematical; one item that turned out to be a Mathlib leaf (def-pc-geometrically-irreducible = `GeometricallyConnected`) was given status `mathlib` by the lead, since the review script had required `split`; the medium workers make more leaves of primitives (real power, complex absolute value, split epimorphism) than the xhigh workers, which recorded those in the searches instead; one deleted an uncommitted duplicate left by its interrupted predecessor. Reports are shorter but complete. The third account hit its limit after about 75 minutes with 19 fable-medium items delivered (one found complete on disk after its worker died) and 9 workers dead mid-item; so fable-medium roughly doubled the items per window compared with fable-xhigh, and the limit still arrived within the hour.

**Pilot numbers, opus at effort `medium` (batch 4, started 07:54 UTC, human's decision in SETUP.md).** 69 items delivered so far, all passed review: wall time 50–634 s (median 205 s), 45k–209k worker tokens (median 85k), 0–11 new nodes per item (median 3, 208 in all). Against fable-medium (median 282 s, 99k, 2.5 new nodes) opus-medium is about the same in time and tokens and makes more nodes per item; against fable-xhigh (median ~720 s, ~170k) it is a third of the time and half the tokens. Proofs: 18 statements proved (median 239 s, 99k tokens): seven follow a document step by step (the geometric-connectedness criterion from Stacks 0387/054N/0363; the constructibility of the constant coefficient objects and of the structure direct images from Bhatt–Scholze, cited by number and page; the étale-origin comparison from Stacks 09AK/09AL; the trace–determinant identity from Milne LEC §27 and Deligne (1.5); the proper-base-change corollary from Stacks 0DDF and SGA 4 XII 5.2, where the blueprint proof had cited the wrong proposition), each with the lemmas the source leaves implicit made into nodes; the others are written by the agent where literature/ has no textbook or the source gives no proof (matrix rank under field extension, the reciprocal polynomial, root multiplicities of products, the reciprocal-root factorization, the coefficients of the reverse characteristic polynomial, the subfields of an algebraic closure of a finite field, the Frobenius transport isomorphisms), each a complete argument whose steps name the Mathlib lemmas that carry them. On review the opus proofs are as complete as the fable ones and as faithful to the sources where a source exists; they are terser (steps of two or three sentences), their elementary judgments were all right on inspection, and their reports are plainer. Quality of the definition splits: as complete as fable's (every notion named; claims inside definitions moved out into statement nodes; hidden existence theorems and inverse-system maps the blueprint left unsaid made explicit); Deligne (Weil II) and Bhatt–Scholze read with pdftotext and cited by page; Stacks places spot-checked correct; one opus worker found and corrected six wrong Stacks tags its fable-medium predecessor had written (verified); two corrected the lead's own brief against the source (no Karoubi completion in the rationalization; Mathlib's ConstantSheaf.lean is not the constant sheaf); one corrected a covering condition of the blueprint against Bhatt–Scholze; one replaced the blueprint's definition of Weil complexes (an undefined localization) by the pairs (K, θ) that Deligne and every user of the node use (accepted, ledger). What the review rejected or corrected: nothing rejected; two duplicates of nodes created the same day by another worker (the pro-étale inverse image; the weakly étale morphism) were dropped or merged by the lead with citations repointed; two claims a worker had kept in a definition that does not need them were removed by the lead with their nodes; four new lemmas that nothing cited yet were flagged unreachable (three since reached and unflagged); one worker ran another worker's scratchpad script by accident, with no damage (brief fixed). Statement errors found and corrected by opus workers against the sources: the refinement comparison applied a lemma to the wrong object (the étale-origin witness was missing); the Q̄_ℓ clause of cor-galois-lisse-representations rested on a convention no node defines; a covering condition of the pro-étale site; the sheaf Hom needed commutative rings; Weil complexes were an undefined localization. No opus worker died. Two opus workers found sorry-free declarations of slopmaster2 that state their nodes exactly and that the blueprint had not named (Fatou's lemma; the finite-coordinate detection lemma; the symmetric alternating-sum parity; the compact-support candidate; compactifications and their refinements): attached at review, those nodes are now closed.

**Pilot numbers, opus at effort `medium` on the target (batch 5, started 11:23 UTC; `local/target-stats.txt`).** 5 items (the hand-picked key nodes), all passed review: wall time 299–540 s (median 376 s), 123k–181k worker tokens, 14 new nodes. Corrected by the lead: a wrong Lean attachment found by a worker (def-weil-pbc-property: `IsBaseChangeIso`, rejected), and on the target two citations of statements the Lean uses but the step does not, moved one level down. Nothing rejected. The unit records `realized` fields were filled correctly by all five; two workers each found Lean declarations for other nodes, which the lead verified with the full sorry map and attached.

## Plan

Written by the launcher: the names of the sessions of the project.

One session, group `L`: the maintainer and the only lead, started 2026-10-09. It gives items to at most 5 workers at a time (sub-agents, model opus, effort medium; earlier in the day 10 fable workers at xhigh, then medium, then 10 opus workers), each splitting one node by one level, reviews each delivery, and commits after every batch.
